# TASKS.md — live task list

One row per task; the table is the current truth, rewritten in place as
states change. Statuses: `pending` → `in_progress` → `done`, or
`blocked` (always say what gates it). User-gated tasks do not start
without the user's explicit go. Evidence links make every status auditable.

Seeded from HANDOFF §8 (2026-09-06); TASKS.md owns live status from now on.

plan docs/plans/2026-09-19-desert-ant-recreation-da1-langid-plan.md. Ladder (feasibility-ranked):

BEFORE training (A0 discipline); CPU-only -> zero single-GPU contention by construction.

| 118 | U-1c prompt-perturbation robustness test (user-reported: editing example prompts breaks generation) | `done` | H2 CONFIRMED, H1 NOT supported. Pre-registered before running (research/EXPERIMENTS.md U-1c). 8 unseen + 8 memorised prompts x 4 arms (original / drop a word / UI-synonym / `Can you please ` prefix), gate-identical decoding, perturbations VERIFIED APPLIED (A1 8/8, A2 7/8, A3 8/8). Unseen arms are FLAT: valid 0.000 in all four, mean F1 0.565-0.625 (spread 0.06, inside the pre-registered 0.15 band). Every MEMORIZED arm: valid 1.000 / F1 0.902 / 0 issues, bit-identical across arms - but the surviving test prompts are formulaic 5-9 word strings and the edits are semantically NULL, so that arm is uninformative about memorization and is NOT cited as evidence of it. ONE REAL EFFECT, length not semantics: the prefix arm lifts truncation 0.50 -> 0.75, elements 4.8 -> 6.0, issues 7.2 -> 10.1, because a longer prompt makes the model write longer AND directly shrinks the budget (cap = ctx - prompt - 4). => output length is the binding constraint, so ctx 512 -> 1024 is the highest-value fix. CAVEAT: n=8 per arm; absolute F1 (0.57-0.63) is not comparable to the 336-prompt gate level (0.799). The user observation was CORRECT but misdiagnosed - root cause was the GUI serving memorised train-split chips from an archetype-ordered corpus, fixed in the same session. |

VALIDITY FAILED THE 0.90 BAR and is recorded as a regression. Root cause MEASURED via scratch/why_invalid.py on 8 of the gate own prompts: 4-of-8 TRUNCATED at ctx 512 with no EOS (gen_tok 468/450/475/463 vs a 468 cap), and the largest clean-termination class is binding_path_missing (11 of 34 issues) - the model learned the BIND surface form but not the obligation to declare the path in STATE, plus duplicate_key 7 from repetition loops and prop_unknown_key 6. F1 scores element TYPES, validity scores WIRING - that is why 0.80 and 0.037 coexist without contradiction. NEXT (needs pre-registration, in expected-value order): ctx 512->1024, then a codec change making bindings self-contained (FORMAT change = user sign-off + catalog re-freeze), then audit prop_unknown_key. NOT more data. Full detail in research/EXPERIMENTS.md. |

Net: E-74 = TYPED SPECIALIST (0.7712 unseen typed, past Laya-FT 0.766, Brier 0.1009, G3 by construction) NOT the encoder-replacement; E-75 (E-70 positional continuation on new corpus) remains the sanctioned scaling line. The model's own `box_thresh: 0.6` is incompatible with the dilation (3.2 boxes/page, 0.404); swept 0.0/0.3/0.5/0.6/0.7 -> adopted **0.3** (0.0 gives 0.933 at 10.0 boxes/page). Unit suite **26/26 PASS**; VRAM peak **1.84 GiB at batch 8** vs 6.97 GiB free. Full run launched 2026-10-04 (job `pwsh-25`) - **its reported Hmean was meaningless**: the eval decoded shrunk components against full GT boxes and binarized with the learned threshold map; corrected re-score of `best.pt` = H 0.20-0.24. **Ceiling analysis (decoding perfect target maps, 40 val pages / 2860 PAWs, IoU 0.5, min_side 6): DBNet shrunk-box + component decode caps at 78.6% at stride 2 - below the gate - because 50.87% of PAWs share bbox area and merge into one component. CRAFT-style Gaussian region scores (sigma = k*size, decoded by a constant) give 92.5% at stride 2 (k=0.20) and 96.9% at stride 1 (k=0.15).** Adopted CRAFT at stride 2 / k=0.20 with no architecture change (prob head = region score, thresh head kept as an auxiliary task); `CRAFTLoss` = weighted MSE, since a focal loss would flatten the Gaussian and break the size decode. Relaunched 2026-10-04 (job `pwsh-28`, `runs/stage2_paw_craft/`; the DBNet baseline in `runs/stage2_paw/` is kept intact). **A THIRD eval bug found+fixed (2026-10-04 (i)): `CRAFT_BLOB_SCALE` = 2*sqrt(2 ln 2) is the 0.5-level-set constant but the eval binarized at `bin_thresh=0.3`, costing 17.8 points even on perfect maps (74.45 vs 92.27). Fixed via `craft_blob_scale(thresh)` + `CRAFT_DECODE_THRESH=0.5`. With the fixed decode the killed run is at H 45.90 val / 46.36 train (not the 3.14 it printed) => UNDERFIT; a 2-page overfit reaches H 89-91% => the architecture is capable and AMP is innocent. Relaunched `pwsh-29` in `runs/stage2_paw_craft2/`, 30 epochs (was 20).** **GATE FAILED 2026-10-04 (j): best Hmean 54.89% (epoch 25, `pwsh-29`, 30 epochs) vs the 90% gate; final 54.43 (P 64.86 R 46.88).** Diagnosis: the run's logged loss was the prob component only (`stage2_train.py` line 138) - the `thresh` term, never read at eval in craft mode, carried **21-61x** the prob loss (train 0.06306 vs 0.00103), so the backbone was trained mostly for an unused map. The decode is no longer the limit: threshold sweep gives model H 64.71 vs a perfect-map ceiling of 86.28 at tau 0.7 (best at any tau 64.98), a decode-scale sweep shows the 1.86x width inflation is MERGING not a scale bias, and TRAIN H 68.34 vs VAL H 64.71 on 30 pages each shows **UNDERFITTING** - the training fit is the wall. **Run 2 (2026-10-04 (k), `pwsh-52`): GATE FAILED AGAIN at 57.34%** (epoch 27; final 57.02, P 65.42 R 50.53). Zeroing `thresh_weight` + widening 2.56M -> 4.94M bought only **+2.45** over craft2 => **capacity and the unused head were NOT the bottleneck.** Diagnosis: detection is now fine (**centre-in-component coverage 91.20%** on val) and H (57.5%) ~= the singleton coverage (985/1807 = 54.5%) - PAWs with their own component match, PAWs in a merged component do not. The blob is the wrong SHAPE: singletons decode to **25.5 x 25.5 (square)** vs GT 20.4 x 22.1 (tall). **FREE WIN found: anisotropic decode (separate kx/ky) = +12 points**, tau 0.7 kx0.25/ky0.20 -> **69.55** (isotropic 57.52). **STRUCTURAL: the perfect-map ceiling at stride 2 is 92.27% (tau 0.5), so the 90% gate leaves 2.3 points of headroom, and at tau 0.7 - where merging is only 10.1% - the ceiling itself (86.28) is BELOW the gate. A perfect model cannot pass at any threshold with manageable merging.** Also: `CRAFTLoss.forward` never reads `target["ignore"]` (inert today). **User chose BOTH (a) and (b) 2026-10-04 (l).** Both implemented and running sequentially (single GPU): (A) stride-1 CRAFT head `runs/stage2_paw_s1` (job `pwsh-4`, ceiling 96.91%), then (B) CenterNet-style centre heatmap + w/h regression `runs/stage2_paw_cn` (ceiling **98.96%**, measured on perfect targets over all 300 val pages). The anisotropic decode (separate `kx`/`ky`) is implemented in `decode_craft_boxes` and available to both. Two pre-run bugs fixed: `collate_pages` was not mode-aware (`KeyError: 'prob'`), and `eval_interval` was a dead config key (the trainer evaluated every epoch).** **RUN A (stride 1) FINISHED 2026-10-04 (m): GATE FAIL at 59.86%** (epoch 29, `pwsh-6`, P 67.62 R 53.70, 5,008,338 params) - +2.52 over craft3's 57.34%, still climbing at the last epoch. Three levers (capacity, dead head, resolution) each bought 2-3 points => the residual is the OPTIMIZATION, not the architecture. **RUN B (CenterNet-style) LAUNCHED 2026-10-04 (m) as `pwsh-7` in `runs/stage2_paw_cn/`** (ceiling 98.96%).** | `arabic_ocr/src/data/paw_targets.py` + `synthetic_pages.py` + `models/paw_detector.py` + `training/detection.py`; `arabic_ocr/scripts/stage2_train.py`; `arabic_ocr/configs/stage2_paw.yaml`; `scratch/stage2_unit.py`; `runs/stage2_synth/` + `runs/stage2_paw/`

AMENDED RULE: abort if the card muharaf_test raw exceeds 30.00 at any eval (unambiguous collapse), or exceeds 26.00 at any eval at or after the LR peak (step >= 19,872). Rationale for continuing: continuing to the LR peak costs ~50 min, while aborting a 20-epoch schedule at 4% during its warmup costs the whole run. See E-107. VERDICT WORKFLOW: see MEMORY.md ("Verdict workflow for a CTC checkpoint") - cache logits, tune delta on muharaf_val only via blank_penalty_tune.py, then compare its per-set delta_star raw CERs to G1/G2/G3. Do NOT read the decode_ctc_lm.py GATES line: its G1/G2/G3 names refer to different quantities on different sets. DISK BUDGET for the verdict (measured 2026-10-07): one cached tag at h96 is 357.7 MB (5 npz: khatt_teklia_test 208 MB, muharaf_test 196 MB, plus the rest); --keep-eval-ckpts leaves 66 checkpoints x ~19 MB = ~1.25 GB; disk free 10.5 GB. Caching ~4 candidate checkpoints plus every checkpoint is ~2.7 GB, so it fits - but cache candidates a few at a time and reuse the existing teacher cache for the baseline rather than re-caching it. The stale teacher_h128/h160/h192 caches (1.78 GB) are from the closed E-102 height sweep and are the first reclaim candidate if space gets tight - NOT deleted, reported only. STOP-RULE AMENDMENT 2 (2026-10-07, recorded at step ~10,900 of 66,240, BEFORE the step-19,872 LR peak - outcome still unknown). MEASURED: the teacher's own card (finetune_muharaf.py --eval-only --ckpt <teacher> --eval-limit 400 - NOTE --eval-only reads --ckpt, not --init-ckpt) scores muharaf_test raw 26.40 (arabic_ocr/runs/teacher_card/scorecard_eval.json). Amendment 1's post-peak clause ("abort if card muharaf_test raw > 26.00 at step >= 19,872") is therefore BELOW the teacher's own score - it would have aborted the teacher. That is a calibration error, not a judgment call, so it is withdrawn. The stop rule is now a single measured collapse test: ABORT if the card muharaf_test raw exceeds 30.00 at ANY eval (+3.6 pp over the teacher's 26.40, +3.3 pp over this run's own floor of 26.72 - unambiguous collapse, not noise). Honest note: this is the SECOND amendment to a threshold I set without calibration; both were my errors and both are recorded rather than quietly dropped. TEACHER CARD BASELINE (400-line subset, no blank penalty, h96) for apples-to-apples reference: muharaf_test 26.40 raw / 13.03 norm, khatt_teklia_test 40.87 / 39.52, isippt_bench 62.09 / 62.33, khatt_bench 41.81 / 40.55. Against that baseline Plan A at step 8000 is +0.82 pp WORSE on muharaf_test (27.22) but -2.96 pp BETTER on khatt_teklia_test (37.91 vs 40.87) - the direction G1 needs. [Full-set reference table, card-vs-fullset bias, and the workflow validation -> E-109 in research/EXPERIMENTS.md.] [Card-vs-fullset calibration and the step-1000..14000 trajectory -> E-108/E-109; current cards are read live from arabic_ocr/runs/stage06b/train_log.jsonl.] CHECKPOINT-SELECTION RULE, PRE-REGISTERED (2026-10-07, BEFORE Plan A finishes; outcome UNKNOWN) - so that "Plan A best checkpoint" is never chosen post hoc after seeing results. G1 is the binding gate, so selection is by khatt_teklia_test; but the full-set delta-tuned verdict costs one 358 MB logits cache per checkpoint, so all ~66 checkpoints cannot be evaluated (~23 GB against 10.4 GB free). RULE: (1) SHORTLIST = the FINAL checkpoint (the only one that has seen the whole annealed schedule, and the most likely winner) UNION the 3 checkpoints with the lowest khatt_teklia_test CARD raw, subject to muharaf_test card raw <= 30.00. (2) Compute the full-set delta-tuned (muharaf_val ONLY) verdict for those <=4 checkpoints and report ALL of them, plus every checkpoint card value, so the selection is auditable. (3) Report the best G1 in the shortlist together with its G2/G3, and state explicitly that the shortlist was chosen on the 400-line card. CAVEAT recorded up front: the card is noisy across checkpoints (khatt_teklia_test card swung 28.40-44.27 over this run), so a minimum over 66 card samples carries selection bias - the final checkpoint is included precisely so the honest answer does not depend on cherry-picking. G2 RISK recorded up front: the teacher PASSES G2 (full-set 22.11 raw, 21.47 with delta=1.00) while Plan A muharaf_test card drifts from +0.32 to +1.44 pp over the teacher card, so A may trade G2 away WITHOUT reaching G1; if that happens it must be reported as a FAIL, not hidden behind the khatt gain. STOP-RULE AMENDMENT 3 (2026-10-07, recorded at step ~23,700 of 66,240, BEFORE the rule fires; outcome unknown). WHY: the amended 30.00 threshold is a single-set ABSOLUTE value and the run is now climbing toward it purely by making the trade the run exists to make - khatt_teklia_test card improved to 23.87 (-17.00 pp vs the teacher card) while muharaf_test card drifted to 28.07 (+1.67 pp over the teacher), on a ~+0.6 pp per 7k-step slope that projects to ~31.8 by step 66,240. A model reading khatt at 23.87 is not destroyed; it is specialised. The 30.00 rule would therefore abort a run that is winning the binding gate - a FALSE POSITIVE. PRINCIPLE, stated explicitly because it is the reason to revise rather than obey: the cost of a false abort is losing the whole run, while the cost of NOT aborting a bad run is nothing at all (the verdict is reported honestly either way). An abort rule must therefore have a very low false-positive rate, and a threshold only 1.67 pp above the model s own baseline does not. NEW RULE (tied to the measured teacher baselines and to the real failure mode, destruction rather than trade): abort if EITHER set s card raw exceeds its measured teacher card by more than 10 pp - i.e. muharaf_test card > 36.40 or khatt_teklia_test card > 50.87. That is unambiguous destruction (option (D) sat at 92-98 CER); at step 23,700 muharaf is 1.67 pp over the teacher and khatt is 17.00 pp UNDER it, so neither is remotely close. HONEST NOTE: this is the THIRD revision of this threshold, and the pattern is itself the finding - I set three uncalibrated absolute thresholds in a row, each time without measuring the starting model on the exact eval subset first. Amendments 1 and 2 were single-set absolutes that ignored that (i) this card reads ~4.3 pp harsher than the full set for muharaf and (ii) the run legitimately trades in-domain muharaf for out-of-domain khatt. All three are recorded rather than quietly dropped.

Out `arabic_ocr/runs/stage06c`.  **PRE-REGISTERED 2026-10-07 before launch.** Trigger: run only if Plan A (row 147) leaves at least one gate failing. Why DEPTH-only: the state_dict dump shows frontend.* and height_attention.* are independent of d_model while every encoder.layers.*, norm, classifier and projection parameter depends on it. Deepening therefore copies ALL 86 source state_dict entries verbatim (exact=86, fresh=48 for L=8; exact=86, fresh=120 for L=14); widening block-copies only 53 of 86. Measured by grow_ctc_model.py --verify: depth-only 4->8 and 4->14 both give max|delta| 0.000e+00 (bit-identical function), while widening to d=384 gives max|delta| 12.21 (L=8) and 12.89 (L=4) - as large as a fresh init, so it would repeat option (D) failure (E-104). Widening cannot be made function-preserving here because LayerNorm normalises across all d_model dimensions. Design: init = Plan A best checkpoint (chosen by delta tuned on muharaf_val), grown depth-only 4 -> 14 encoder layers at d_model 256 / ffn 1024 (4,245,671 -> 12,143,271 params, 2.86x), then continue with the same authors recipe (augment light, train blank penalty 0.2, OneCycleLR 5e-5, batch 8, wd 1e-4, clip 5.0) for 8 epochs, eval every 1000, keep-eval-ckpts. Gates: G1 khatt teklia test raw <= 21.00; G2 muharaf test raw <= 23.11; G3 khatt_bench <= 29.54 / isippt <= 58.34; delta tuned on muharaf_val ONLY; raw and norm_ar both reported. Stop rule: abort if muharaf_test raw > 26.00 at any eval. Exact launch command - pass --reallines-repeat 0, because otherwise reallines_train is silently INCLUDED and steps/epoch becomes 4,479 instead of 3,312: finetune_muharaf.py --init-ckpt <grown.pt> --augment light --train-blank-penalty 0.2 --scheduler onecycle --lr 5e-5 --epochs 8 --batch 8 --accum 1 --khatt-repeat 1 --reallines-repeat 0 --target-height 96 --eval-every 1000 --eval-limit 400 --keep-eval-ckpts --stage 0.6c-planB --out arabic_ocr/runs/stage06c. Estimated ~1.9 steps/s (encoder compute scales 3.5x over the 4-layer 5.3 steps/s) = ~3.8 h for 8 epochs; confirm with a 20-step GPU smoke before the long launch. | E-103; E-104; E-105; E-106; arabic_ocr/scripts/grow_ctc_model.py | REHEARSED 2026-10-07 on a REAL Plan A checkpoint (not the teacher): grow_ctc_model.py --src arabic_ocr/runs/stage06b/ckpt_step014000.pt --out arabic_ocr/runs/grow_test/planA14000_L14.pt --arch-layers 14 --verify -> src d=256 L=4 H=8 F=1024 params=4245671; dst d=256 L=14 H=8 F=1024 params=12143271; transfer exact=86 block=0 fresh=120, new layers [4..13] zero-init=True; verify max|delta| 0.000e+00 (bit-identical function). check_ckpt_reload.py on the grown file -> params 12143271, verdict PASS. Plan A ckpt keys are [charset, epoch, model_config, model_state, stage, step, target_height] (NO optimizer - that lives only in ckpt_last.pt), stage 0.6b-planA, target_height 96. So the B launch path is validated on the actual artifact B will consume, not merely on the teacher. ROUND-BUDGET CONTINGENCY, PRE-REGISTERED 2026-10-07 (before it is needed, so it is not chosen post hoc): the goal this task runs under has a fixed round budget, and B (8 epochs, ~3.9 h) may not fit in what remains after A s verdict. RULE: B is launched as pre-registered above at the full 8 epochs - the epoch count is NOT reduced in advance, because a shorter run is a different experiment and pre-emptively shrinking it would bias the verdict downward. If the budget cannot accommodate the full run, B is evaluated at the LATEST CHECKPOINT REACHED, the verdict reports the exact step and epoch fraction attained, and the report states plainly that B is a PARTIAL run whose verdict is provisional. This keeps the pre-registered experiment intact while guaranteeing the goal can still be answered honestly. Rationale for evaluating rather than discarding: a partial B still answers whether growth helps, and the alternative (reporting nothing) wastes both the compute and the question.

AMENDMENT (2026-10-07, from the TASKS 154 audit, BEFORE launch): ALSO add RESOLUTION-DEGRADATION augmentation in this same step - downsample each line to ~27 px and back to 96 - because the 27 px ISIPPT regime is the measured failure and this needs no new data and no licence. Measured curve at batch 8 (20-40 steps each, `--res-degrade-prob 0.5`): h64 2.15 GiB / 0.17 s/step, h96 4.06 / 0.23, h128 6.61 / 0.44 (reserved 9.49), h160 9.94 / 2.75. Band therefore set to **64-112** (~5.5 GiB projected, inside the measured ~7.4 GiB spill line). Implemented as `--height-band LO,HI`, sampled per BATCH so the collated tensor stays uniform and `collate` never sees mixed heights. Resolution degradation is a separate flag pair `--res-degrade-prob/--res-degrade-band` and is applied AFTER the affine jitter and BEFORE `preprocess_image` upsamples to target_h - so it simulates a low-res SCAN without moving any tensor geometry, unlike a target-height change. So RL did NOT buy its greedy gain with diversity - it recovered both relative to its own init, from 5/8 and 0.25 up to 6/8 and arm A's 0.34, while adding 4 greedy tasks. Evidence: research/raw/e121b_rl_arms_passkdev_cpu.json. **S10 BUILT 2026-10-13 (subagent, Lead-verified)**: per-aspect LoRA + merge now exists as four NEW files - glm-5.3-flash-from-scratch/glm53_flash/lora.py (apply_lora freezes the base; lora_state_dict/save_adapter/load_adapter; merge_lora folds with scale=alpha/r; merge_adapters supports average and ties; count_trainable), scripts/train_aspect_lora.py (one adapter per aspect, masked completion-only CE with the causal shift, atomic saves, prune_checkpoints, progress.jsonl, training-receipt.json, --max-seconds), scripts/merge_loras.py (average/ties + receipt), tests/test_lora.py. Lead re-ran the tests independently: **6 passed, exit 0** (base frozen, forward shape unchanged, merge numerically identical with max abs diff < 1e-4, save/load round-trip, average==elementwise mean and ties finite, lora < total). CPU smoke exit 0 (trainable=lora 6144 of 302152, 1/1 epochs, val_loss 5.0308, atomic checkpoints + prune exercised). DESIGN NOTE: this decoder fuses Q/K/V into one qkv Linear and names the attention output out, so the conventional q_proj/k_proj/v_proj/o_proj targets resolve through a documented alias table to qkv/out (targets=("qkv","out") is equivalent). LIMITATION: adapter.pt / best-adapter.pt are the reloadable artifacts - trainer checkpoints carry LoRA keys and cannot be loaded by runtime.load_checkpoint (strict); no resume path (out of S10 scope). **S10 MEASURED 2026-10-13: structurally SAFE but NOT yet effective.** Trained all 12 core adapters from arm A (runs/e121_ref/checkpoint-0100, sha256 2b0f4303...; 110,592 LoRA params each of 25,841,184; 24 modules; ~2 s each on CPU; 4 tasks/aspect x 3 epochs = 12 training examples per aspect), baked the average and TIES merges (24 modules, max_abs_diff 1.9e-6 / 1.1e-6, ok=true), and measured on TWO held-out batteries in the same session. (1) Legacy dev battery (scratch/bench_aspects.py): base 9/32 -> s10_merged_average 10/32 -> s10_merged_ties 10/32 - so merging twelve adapters did NOT damage the base, unlike the aspects-only RFT which cost 4 tasks; the "one adapter per aspect, then combine" design is confirmed interference-free. (2) NEW aspect-pool battery (scratch/bench_aspect_pool.py, 12 core aspects x 4 = 48 tasks, dev split, greedy, deterministic - two runs byte-identical): arm A 26/48, s10_merged_average 26/48, s10_merged_ties 26/48, and RL 26/48 - IDENTICAL per aspect for all four arms (sign 4, clamp 4, grade 0, is_palindrome 3, repeat_text 4, first_or_empty 2, count_even 0, count_positive 0, contains_zero 1, lookup 0, key_count 4, has_key 4). So the adapters produced no measurable behavioural change, and RL did not move the aspect pool either. Cause under test: 12 training examples per aspect is far too few - a scaled retrain at --tasks-per-family 200 --epochs 5 is running into runs/s10_adapters_big. **E-122 VERDICT 2026-10-13: reference SFT was simply UNDER-TRAINED - 6x exposure took the held-out legacy battery from 9/32 to 32/32.** Same data (scripts/reference_sft_bytes.py corpus, 400 instances/source), same init (runs/e117_byte/checkpoint-5000), same lr 1e-4 cosine, only steps 100 -> 600 (19,660,800 tokens, 4010.152 s, peak 3.005 GiB, no spill, val_loss 0.2052 vs arm A 0.2772). BOTH retained checkpoints score 32/32 - all eight families 4/4 (absolute, double, even, increment, list_sum, nonnegative, reverse, square), up from 9/32. LEAKAGE CONTROL (scratch/leak_check_e122.py): dev prompt-in-corpus 0/32 and prompt+body-in-corpus 0/32; body-in-corpus 32/32 is expected and harmless because each family's reference body is a CONSTANT string the corpus contains from its own pretrain instances. evaluate_source EXECUTES every completion against the dev task's own cases, so each pass is earned. This SUPERSEDES arm A as the ladder base and SATURATES the legacy battery - the frontier is now the aspect pool (grade, count_even, count_positive, lookup still 0/4). **E-122 ASPECT POOL 2026-10-14: the falsifiable prediction is CONFIRMED - exposure fixes the single-expression aspects, and FOUR constructs stay at ZERO.** Same three arms on the aspect-pool battery (scratch/bench_aspect_pool.py, 12 core aspects x 4 = 48 dev tasks, greedy, deterministic): e121ref (100 steps) 26/48 -> E-122 checkpoint-0330 (best) **32/48** -> checkpoint-0600 (last) 29/48. Per aspect - FIXED BY EXPOSURE: first_or_empty 2->4, contains_zero 1->4, is_palindrome 3->4 (all single-expression bodies: text[:1], 0 in x, x == x[::-1]). STILL ZERO AT EVERY EXPOSURE: **grade, count_even, count_positive, lookup** - all four require a multi-statement / comprehension / conditional-expression form. So the constraint is the CONSTRUCT, not the exposure, and the fix is different task templates (the per-part playground), not more steps. ALSO: the best checkpoint beats the last (32 vs 29; sign 4->3, clamp 4->3, is_palindrome 4->3), so val_loss retention picked the right artifact, and the legacy 32/32 is saturated while the aspect pool still discriminates. **BLOCKER FOUND 2026-10-14 (evaluator capability default): every RFT/RL harvest so far ran with allow_loops=false and allow_attributes=false.** train_rft.py builds Capabilities(allow_loops=args.allow_loops, allow_attributes=args.allow_attributes) at l.220-222 from two SEPARATE store_true flags; --tier only selects the TASK POOL (l.211) and does not touch capabilities. The earlier receipts confirm the default was in force. So the harvest could not accept ANY loop or attribute solution - exactly the forms count_even, count_positive (loop/comprehension) and lookup (subscript/.get) require. That is a MECHANICAL reason RL could not fix them, independent of policy sharpness: pass@k measured 0 partly because the EVALUATOR refused the construct, not only because the model could not emit it. Corrected run: runs/e122c_rft_cap, --pool all --tier all --allow-loops --allow-attributes --sandbox on --sandbox-timeout 2.0 from E-122 best. The earlier RL 16/32 legacy gain still stands (those families need no loops), but 'RL cannot learn the aspect constructs' must be re-measured under corrected capabilities. **FALSIFIED the same day by the corrected run - runs/e122c_rft_cap.** With --allow-loops --allow-attributes --tier all, round 1 harvested 640 samples (sandbox_requests 640, restarts 0, timeouts 0, keep_rate 0.795 = 509 pairs) and count_even, count_positive, grade, lookup were STILL 0/32 EACH, while every other source was 32/32 (sign 29/32). So the capability flags were NOT the blocker and the evaluator was never refusing those constructs: with loops and attributes permitted the model still produced no valid solution. The four constructs are genuinely outside this base's reach under sampling, so pass@k=0 stands as a property of the MODEL - RFT/RL cannot fix them and the reference-SFT route (the playground ladders) is the only supported fix. Keep the flags anyway: they are correct and were silently wrong before. NOTE the new harness disagrees with the recorded keep-rate pre-flight on first_or_empty (2/4 greedy dev vs 0/32 pretrain T=0.35) - reported, explained by split+decoding, not a harness bug (all 48 reference bodies pass; empty completion never passes). Evidence: research/raw/e121b_rl_all_train.log. |

**Pre-registration, fixed BEFORE the first GPU launch (verbatim):**

**PRE-REGISTERED 2026-10-08 BEFORE the first GPU launch.** The clone is tracked as a nested gitlink (HEAD 96d9c7c; never pushed to vukrosic). PORT: CUDA autocast is fp16 + GradScaler because Turing sm_75 has no bf16 (a bf16 request now RAISES below sm_80 instead of silently downgrading); `torch==2.5.1` is unpinned so the parent `.venv` (torch 2.14.0+cu126) is reused and no second environment is created; zero-flag auto-resume from `<checkpoint>/state.pt` (optimizer + GradScaler + RNG) with incremental `progress.jsonl` and a receipt rebuilt from disk; `--vram-cap-gib` allocator ceiling + peak-allocated-vs-device-total spill detector (AGENTS 20.3); `--grad-accum`; `scripts/vram_probe.py`; `tests/test_resume.py` (13 tests PASS on CPU). RECIPE IS UPSTREAM'S, UNCHANGED: pretrain 400 steps / batch 32 / seq 128 / lr 3e-4 / seed 42 / ckpt 100,200,400; then RL initialized from **checkpoint-0100** (the with-headroom checkpoint the upstream reproduce block uses, not the best one) / groups 96 / group-size 16 / tasks-per-family 32 / families increment,double,even / temperature 0.35 / lr 5e-5 / train-scope last-block-head / reward-mode binary / seed 31415 / ckpt 24,48,72,96; then dev pass@1 at ckpt 0/100/200/400, confirm greedy at pretrain-0100 vs rl-0096, and confirm pass@8 (per-family 8, samples 8, temperature 0.35, seed 8675309) for the 3 trained families and all 8. VALIDATION CRITERIA (fixed before launch; upstream claim in brackets): **V1** dev pass@1 (8 tasks) rises from <=2/8 at ckpt-0100 to >=6/8 at ckpt-0400 [upstream 2/8 -> 8/8]; **V2** greedy confirm (24 tasks) rises from <=2/24 pre-RL to >=12/24 post-RL, paired gain >= +8 tasks, one-sided McNemar p < 0.05 [0/24 -> 16/24, 16 gains 0 losses, p = 3.05e-5]; **V3** pass@8 confirm gain >= +6 tasks on the 3 trained families [9/24 -> 19/24; 13/192 -> 88/192] and all-8-families gain >= +4 [25/64 -> 34/64]; **V4** the per-family pattern reproduces (increment and double become reliable, `even` stays unreliable, at least one untrained family regresses greedily). A result below the criteria is a reportable FAIL; every deviation must be attributed to fp16-vs-bf16, torch 2.14-vs-2.5.1, or the device.

**PRE-REGISTERED 2026-10-08 BEFORE THE FIRST GPU LAUNCH (TASKS 159 / E-114, arm A4 - the fp32-control RL arm). Verbatim:**

CONTEXT. E-113 (TASKS 158) refuted the headroom explanation: the RL regime was already matched (the published bf16 step-100 init produced 417/1536 exact rollouts on the RL schedule; our fp16 step-100 init produced 406/1536, 2.6% apart) and the endpoint still failed - greedy gain +7 (5/24 -> 12/24) against the published +16 (0/24 -> 16/24). The leading remaining suspect is the RL step itself under fp16/torch-2.14: a lower per-group conversion rate from reward into greedy correctness, plausibly because fp16 activation range in the last block limits what a head-only update can express. E-113 varied the init, not the dtype/optimizer path, so it cannot separate this. A4 varies ONLY that.

DESIGN. Arm A4 = the E-112/A2 arm with the dtype path changed and nothing else. Init runs/pretrain/checkpoint-0100/model.pt (sha256 44d740f77fa0398bd2dc4b3af8d60ec7182e050c84643920550655e8be2f5984, 103,045,899 B - the same file A2 trained from), output runs/rl-regime-a4, --groups 96 --checkpoints 24,48,72,96 --group-size 16 --tasks-per-family 32 --families increment,double,even --temperature 0.35 --learning-rate 5e-5 --train-scope last-block-head --reward-mode binary --seed 31415 --vram-cap-gib 4.10. The single change: --amp-dtype fp16 -> fp32 (amp_dtype(cuda, fp32) is None, so no GradScaler; the guards are covered by tests/test_resume.py::test_amp_dtype_is_fp16_on_cuda_and_disabled_on_cpu). Manipulation checks, enforced in code before the run and logged: (i) the init file hash must equal the constant above; (ii) the receipt's schedule_sha256 must equal 0c2327ee7cd08db42a32ea78e69e590d80592d5068d7d6deca216c8b948fbc2e (A1/A2/A3 all carry it, so the task schedule is identical); (iii) the receipt's amp_dtype must read fp32. Baselines are E-112's own checkpoint-0100 evals (confirm-greedy-pretrain-0100.json, confirm-pass8-pretrain-0100.json, confirm-pass8-all-pretrain-0100.json), reused rather than re-measured so A2 and A4 are paired on the identical before-file.

PREDICTIONS. P1 (primary): if fp16 is the cause, A4 greedy confirm gain >= +12 tasks (published +16; A2 +7). P2: A4 pass@8 gain over the 3 trained families >= +8 (published +10; A2 +5). P3: A4 greedy endpoint >= 13/24, strictly above A2's 12/24. P4 (manipulation): A4's init-time exact-rollout count on the RL schedule is reported next to A2's 406/1536 - fp32 changes the sampled tokens, so exact equality is NOT required; a large departure must be reported as a change in the sampling distribution, not as noise.

FALSIFICATION. A4 greedy gain <= +8 (endpoint near A2's 12/24) refutes the fp16-conversion hypothesis: dtype is exonerated and the suspect moves to the factors A2/A4 share (the upstream torch 2.5.1 vs 2.14 optimizer/autograd path, the confirm-set difficulty, or the published device's bf16 numerics). A4 gain >= +12 supports the hypothesis and makes fp16 the actionable lever for this port.

COST / SCOPE. One RL run (A1 was 341.5 s fp16 at 0.933 GiB peak; fp32 expected 1.5-3x slower, peak to be reported against the 4.10 GiB cap) plus three after-evals reusing E-112's before-files. Single GPU; cap 4.10 GiB = 54% of the 7,721 MiB free at launch (idle GPU, 274 MiB used). Scope: 25.7M params, context 192, one init, one dtype change - nothing here speaks to larger models. The nested clone stays a gitlink and is never pushed to vukrosic. Licensed at one machine: torch 2.14.0+cu126, Python 3.12.9, Windows, Quadro RTX 4000 8 GiB.

## Pre-registration - TASKS 160 / E-115 (A4 learning-curve read), written 2026-10-08 before the first GPU launch

Question: is the A4 RL endpoint a step-budget plateau, or is the confirm curve still rising at
the 96-group budget?

Design: EVAL ONLY - no training. Re-evaluate the checkpoints A4 already saved
(runs/rl-regime-a4/checkpoint-{0024,0048,0072,0096}) against the SAME before-files E-114 used
(runs/eval/confirm-greedy-pretrain-0100.json, confirm-pass8-pretrain-0100.json). Nothing else
changes: same evaluate.py / evaluate_passk.py code, same confirm split, same 24-task greedy set,
same 3 trained families for pass@8, same seeds. The 96-group point is REUSED from E-114's own
receipts (comparison-a4-greedy.json, comparison-a4-pass8.json) rather than re-measured, so the
curve's endpoint and E-114's endpoint are the same measurement by construction.

Measurements: greedy solved/24 at 24/48/72/96 groups; pass@8 solved/24 (3 trained families) at
24/48/72/96 groups; per-family allocation at each point.

Pre-registered reading (decided before the run):
- PLATEAU if greedy solved(96) == greedy solved(72) (the last 24 groups buy nothing), or if the
  curve is non-monotone. -> the step budget is not the limiter; the surviving explanation is the
  recipe/scale rather than more groups, and A is answered 'plateau'.
- STILL RISING if greedy solved(96) > greedy solved(72) and the curve is monotone
  non-decreasing. -> longer RL (e.g. 192 groups) is a live lever and must be pre-registered as
  its own arm before any further GPU spend.
- Either way report pass@8 for the same checkpoints: a greedy plateau with a rising pass@8 means
  the policy is still improving in a way greedy cannot see.

Cost/rig: 6 evals (~3 greedy at ~82 s, 3 pass@8 at ~104 s) on the Quadro RTX 4000, cap
4.10 GiB, single GPU, no training. Expected ~10 min.

Falsifier for the PLATEAU reading: any greedy gain after 72 groups (solved(96) > solved(72)).

OUTCOME (added after the run, 2026-10-08): the literal rule fired PLATEAU on the non-monotonicity clause (greedy 8, 8, 7, 12), but the pre-registered falsifier was met - the final window gained +5 greedy (7 -> 12), the largest jump on the curve - and pass@8 was already flat from 72 groups (12, 12, 15, 15). Substantive reading: NOT SATURATED on greedy, saturated on pass@8 from 72. The monotonicity clause was mis-specified (a 1-task dip is 4.2 pp here); the STILL-RISING branch's action applies. See research/2026-10-08_glm53_flash_a4_learning_curve.md.

## Pre-registration - TASKS 161 / E-116 (A6: the 192-group RL arm), written 2026-10-08 before the first GPU launch

Question: E-115 showed the A4 greedy curve is not step-capped at 96 groups (8, 8, 7, 12 at
24/48/72/96) while 3-family pass@8 was already flat from 72 (12, 12, 15, 15). Does the greedy
climb continue with a longer budget, and is the published 16/24 endpoint reachable by spending
more steps rather than by changing the recipe?

Design: one arm, identical to A4 in every respect except the step budget - same init
(runs/pretrain/checkpoint-0100, sha256 44d740f77fa0398bd2dc4b3af8d60ec7182e050c84643920550655e8be2f5984),
same recipe (--group-size 16, --tasks-per-family 32, --families increment,double,even,
--temperature 0.35, --learning-rate 5e-5, --train-scope last-block-head, --reward-mode binary,
--seed 31415, --amp-dtype fp32, cap 4.10 GiB), --groups 192 with --checkpoints 96,144,192.

Gates (decided before the run):
- G1 EQUIVALENCE: sha256(runs/rl-regime-a6/checkpoint-0096/model.pt) vs
  sha256(runs/rl-regime-a4/checkpoint-0096/model.pt). If equal, A6 is a pure continuation of the
  A4 trajectory and the 96-group point reuses A4's receipts unchanged. If not equal, the schedule
  or the loop depends on the total budget, so the 96-group point is re-evaluated on A6's own
  checkpoint and A6 is recorded as 'longer budget, rescaled schedule' rather than a pure
  extension - both outcomes are informative and neither is a failure.
- G2 CURVE: greedy solved/24 at 96/144/192 against the same before-file. STILL RISING if
  solved(192) > solved(96); SATURATED if solved(192) == solved(96).
- G3 REACH: greedy solved(192) >= 16/24 means the published endpoint is reachable by step budget
  alone, so the A4 gap was a budget gap rather than a recipe ceiling.
- P1 (continues): solved(192) > solved(96).
- P2 (reaches published): solved(192) >= 16/24.
- Falsifier: solved(192) <= solved(96) - the 96-group budget was NOT the limiter, and the
  remaining explanation is the recipe/scale itself (the bf16 device is untestable on sm_75 and
  the published-torch control is excluded by user decision).
- Also report pass@8 (3 trained families) at 144 and 192: a rising greedy with flat pass@8 (or
  the reverse) bounds where the policy actually improves.

Cost/rig: ~2x the A4 RL run (A4: 96 groups in 464.5 s, 1.495 GiB peak) = ~16 min, plus 4 evals
(greedy ~115 s each, pass@8 ~160 s each) = ~9 min, total ~25 min on the Quadro RTX 4000, cap
4.10 GiB, single GPU, fp32, no spill.

OUTCOME (added after the run, 2026-10-08): all three pre-registered gates PASS. G1 equivalence TRUE - sha256(a6/checkpoint-0096) == sha256(a4/checkpoint-0096), so A6 is a pure continuation of A4 and the E-114 step-96 receipts were reused instead of re-measured. G2 still-rising - greedy solved/24 12 -> 13 -> 16 at 96/144/192 (11/0 at 192, p=0.00098). G3 published parity - 16/24 equals the published greedy endpoint exactly. 3-family pass@8 15 -> 14 -> 17/24, refuting E-115's 'saturated at 72' reading. The post-hoc all-8 probe is flat (30/64 -> 30/64, 8 gains / 8 losses, p=1.0) because the untrained families regress as much as the trained ones gain (square 8 -> 3, reverse 3 -> 0), so the 3-family gain is not free. Residual: the published run needed 96 groups (1,536 rollouts) where we needed 192 (3,072) - a per-group efficiency gap, not a capability gap. See research/2026-10-08_glm53_flash_192_group_rl.md.

## E-117 pre-registration (path 1) - the glm arch on the parent target corpus

PRE-REGISTERED 2026-10-10 BEFORE the first GPU launch.

QUESTION. Can the glm-5.3-flash arch (12L x dim 192, 8 experts top-2 + 1 shared, 4
residual streams; 25.73M total / 9.81M active on the byte vocab) trained on the SAME
corpus as the parent M3 target (CodeSearchNet python: 47,937,963 train tokens / 979,559
val tokens, 46,814 / 956 blocks of 1024) match or beat that target - 226.53M dense
params, val_loss 1.8641 at step 5000, recorded best 1.8512 at step 4000 - at the same
5000-step schedule?

CONTEXT. Two facts fixed the design: (a) the glm line's pretraining corpus is synthetic
(glm53_flash.tasks.pretraining_text samples the 8 RL families), so this is the model's
first real code; (b) runs/target/checkpoint-4000 (best 1.8512) was deleted in the
post-M3 cleanup and no backup zip holds it, so the head-to-head reference is
runs/target/final (step 5000), re-measured in this session because HANDOFF flags the
on-disk eval_report as stale, with 1.8512 reported as the published bar.

ARMS (single GPU, sequential, fp16, seed 42, checkpoints 1000/2500/5000).
- A-BYTE (primary): glm arch as-is, byte tokenizer (vocab 260), corpus
  data/target/bytes/{train,val}.bin (CodeLlama detokenization of data/target/tokens,
  re-encoded as ByteTokenizer ids = 4 + utf-8 byte; built by scripts/target_bytes.py),
  ctx 1024 bytes, 5000 steps x batch 32 x 1024 = 163.8M byte positions (~1.0 epoch of
  the corpus, exact ratio from the measured bytes_per_token).
- B-BPE: the same arch with vocab 32768 and the CodeLlama tokenizer, corpus
  data/target/tokens as-is (uint32 ids), ctx 1024 tokens, 5000 steps x 32 x 1024 =
  163.8M tokens (3.42 epochs - the target's exact stream, schedule shape and split).

METRICS. Val loss on the target val shard. A-BYTE is reported in bits/byte
(BPB = loss_nats / ln2 / bytes_per_token, bytes_per_token measured from the val
detokenization) so it is comparable to the target's token loss; B-BPE is reported in
nats/token and perplexity, directly comparable to 1.8641 / 1.8512.

GATES.
- G1 (primary, A-BYTE): BPB(byte arm) < BPB(runs/target/final re-measured) - a 25.7M
  byte-level model beats a 226.53M dense BPE model on bits/byte over the same text.
- G2 (competitive band): BPB(byte arm) <= 1.10 x BPB(target).
- G3 (B-BPE): val_loss(B-BPE) < 1.8641 - the arch beats the target's final at 1/7 the
  parameters; < 1.8512 also beats the recorded step-4000 best.
- G4 (curve): val loss at step 5000 still falling (5000 - 2500 window > 0). If G4 holds,
  the pre-registered continuation is text-exposure parity (17,100 steps = 560M byte
  positions = 3.42 epochs) - A-BYTE only.

FALSIFIER. BPB(byte arm) > 1.5 x BPB(target) AND val_loss(B-BPE) > 2.5 refutes "the glm
arch is competitive on real code at this scale": the next lever is scale (dim/layers),
not data, and the arch is recorded as synthetic-corpus-only.

STATED LIMITATION. A-BYTE at 5000 steps sees ~1.0 epoch where the target saw 3.42; the
schedule shape (steps x sequences x positions) is matched, the text exposure is not.
G4's continuation is how that gets resolved, and it is pre-registered here.

COST / RIG. Probe first (AGENTS 20): 7645 MiB free at pre-registration time, 60% cap =
4587 MiB. A-BYTE and B-BPE are each ~45-75 min (31-32 PFLOPs) at ctx 1024; the target
re-measure is ~2 min; single GPU, no spill, WDDM-spill detector on.
## E-117 addendum 3 (2026-10-10) - fp16 prefix accumulator overflow, fixed before resume [SUPERSEDED - see addendum 4]

INCIDENT: attempt 3 died at step 698 micro 7 with `FloatingPointError: non-finite loss at
step 698 micro 7: 33 skipped micro-batches`. Skips began at step 592 micro 1 (index 4729),
then 676, then cascaded across steps 695-698. The retained-graph fix held (no OOM); the
failure is a sustained fp16 forward overflow.

DIAGNOSIS (measured): a one-shot probe added to the trainer (`--diag-nonfinite`) resumed the
run from checkpoint-0500 and re-ran the exact non-finite micro-batch with a forward hook on
every module. The first non-finite tensor is `layers.10.block.attention.out` (fp16) and the
fp16 loss is non-finite. Root cause: `LinearAttention.forward` accumulated its prefix with
`torch.einsum(...).cumsum(dim=1)` over all 1024 positions in fp16. The prefix is a sum of
non-negative terms, so it grows with position; as the weights sharpen it crosses the fp16
ceiling (65504) -> inf -> non-finite loss. This is why skip frequency rose with training.

FIX (numerical, math-identical): accumulate the prefix in fp32 over 128-position chunks.
Same arithmetic, wider accumulator, and a smaller transient (134 MB fp32 per chunk vs
536 MB fp16 for the whole sequence). Regression test:
`glm-5.3-flash-from-scratch/tests/test_linear_attention_numerics.py` pins forward AND
backward equivalence against the legacy math in fp64.

SCOPE: a stability bug fix, not a recipe change. Batch 4 x accum 8, lr 3e-4 cosine, warmup
150, ctx 1024, vocab 260, cap 4.40 GiB and every E-117 gate are UNCHANGED. The run resumes
from checkpoint-0500; steps 1-500 were produced by the fp16 accumulator, so the arm is a
mixed-accumulator trajectory. Reported as a known limitation, not hidden.

CORRECTION (2026-10-10): the accumulator dtype was not the root cause and this fix alone did
not stop the failure - the resumed run reproduced the identical skip ladder (592 micro 1,
676, cascade 695-702) and died at step 702 with 33 skipped micro-batches. The fp32 control
value from the probe was lost when the diagnostic job's stream buffer was drained; probe
stdout is now always redirected to a file. Addendum 4 records the measured cause.

## E-117 addendum 4 (2026-10-10) - the real cause: autocast downcasts the attention contractions

INCIDENT: four attempts died in the same place. Skips began at step 592 micro 1 (index
4729), then 676, then cascaded across steps 695-702 into `FloatingPointError: non-finite loss
at step 702 micro 1: 33 skipped micro-batches`. The skip pattern is input-driven (at step 698
micros 2-3 were still finite), not weight corruption.

MEASURED CAUSE (three-pass `--diag-nonfinite` probe on the same replay, 2026-10-10):
- Every module output in the fp16 forward stayed finite and SMALL: peak |x| = 8.3 (worst
  module `layers.5.block.attention.qkv`). Nothing approaches the fp16 ceiling (65504), so the
  'fp16 range overflow' story of addendum 3 cannot be the mechanism.
- First non-finite tensor: `layers.10.block.attention.attention_result` (the input to the
  attention out projection), max_abs NaN; the qkv output feeding it is finite (7.13).
- A TorchDispatchMode NaN watch names the first non-finite ATen op: `aten.bmm.default`, inputs
  [fp16, fp16], output shape [3072, 1, 1]. 3072 = batch 4 x chunk 128 x heads 6 - the
  denominator dot product `einsum("bchd,bchd->bch", q, cumsum(k))` in `LinearAttention`.
- The same micro-batch is FINITE in fp32 (loss 1.4686): the failure is dtype-dependent.

MECHANISM: `k = F.elu(k) + 1.0` is non-negative, so `cumsum(k)` is a running prefix whose
magnitude grows with position (~8 x 128 per element inside a chunk). The head_dim reduction
over those terms reaches ~1e5, past fp16's 65504 -> inf -> the
`numerator / denominator.clamp_min(1e-6)` normalisation then returns NaN. The accumulator was
never fp32: einsum and bmm are matmul-family ops, so autocast casts their inputs to the
autocast dtype regardless of what the caller passed. Addendum 3's explicit `.to(fp32)` was
silently undone at dispatch.

FIX: `with torch.autocast(device_type=x.device.type, enabled=False):` around the RoPE / elu /
chunked accumulation in `LinearAttention.forward`; the qkv and out projections stay under
autocast (fp16). The accumulator dtype remains `promote_types(x.dtype, torch.float32)` and the
result is cast back to `x.dtype` before the out projection, so the fp64 and fp16-module tests
both hold.

EVIDENCE: resumed 2026-10-10 from checkpoint-0500 and cleared the entire poison region - step
710, 0 `skip_nonfinite`, 0 `FloatingPointError` (attempts 3 and 4 died at 698 and 702). Losses
are continuous with the pre-fix trajectory (1.77 at step 580 both before and after), so this is
numerical hardening, not a recipe change. Pace unchanged (~4.3-5.2 s/step).

SCOPE: recipe, gates and checkpoints unchanged. Steps 1-500 were produced by the chunked-fp16
forward and steps 501+ by the autocast-disabled one, so the arm is a mixed-numerics trajectory
- reported, not hidden. Regression test:
`glm-5.3-flash-from-scratch/tests/test_linear_attention_numerics.py` (fp64 equivalence,
gradient flow, legacy-overflow, autocast case).
KNOWN GAP: the fp32 control value from the probe was lost when the diagnostic job's stream
buffer was drained (it is the only value not captured).

## E-118 pre-registration (path 2 - RL playground stage 1)

Question: do per-task generated cases, capability tiers and an out-of-process evaluator make
RL on the glm 25M model (a) able to train on aspects the v1 evaluator cannot express, and
(b) measurable per family instead of only in aggregate?

Arms (RL only; no pretrain launch):
- A-ASPECTS: --pool aspects --tier core - 12 new families, cases generated per task.
- A-LEGACY: --pool legacy - the original 8 families with fixed cases (the E-116 control).
- A-TIER: --pool aspects --tier loops --allow-loops --extra-safe-calls range - sandbox only.

Metrics: per-family pass@k (scripts/eval_forgetting_matrix.py), aggregate solved/64,
exact_rollouts, reward_spread, invalid rate, sandbox request/timeout/restart counts; plus
in-process vs sandbox verdict agreement on a fixed candidate corpus.

Gates:
- G1 every generated case is passed by its own reference (unit test, 100% of aspects/splits).
- G2 sandbox verdicts are identical to in-process verdicts on the parity corpus.
- G3 a hanging candidate is killed and the runner recovers (timeout enforced, no hang).
- G4 the aspect pool trains end-to-end: reward_spread > 0 on at least one group and a
  checkpoint is written.

Falsifier: if 32 groups of aspect training never produce reward_spread > 0, generated cases
are too hard for a 25M model and the pool is not a usable RL signal.

Cost/rig: CPU smoke verified 2026-10-10 (2 groups, 6.8 s, aspects + sandbox). A 32-group GPU
RL run is ~10-20 min at the E-116 pace; it waits for E-117 A-BYTE to finish (single GPU).

Stated limitation: the AST allowlist still forbids classes, imports, exceptions and recursion
even at tier loops - the tier is a documented relaxation, not a sandbox escape.
### E-117 addendum (2026-10-10) - restart after an fp16 overflow

The first A-BYTE attempt (job pwsh-10) ran 334 steps to loss 1.97 and then aborted with
FloatingPointError 'non-finite loss at step 334 micro 2'. It was an fp16 forward overflow,
not a data or harness fault; no checkpoint had been written (cadence was 1000/2500/5000), so
the attempt was discarded. The log is kept at runs/e117_byte_failed_attempts.

Deviations from the pre-registration above, all disclosed:
1. train_pretrain.py now skips a non-finite micro-batch (logged as skip_nonfinite), divides
   the step loss by the number of used micro-batches, and aborts after
   --max-skipped-micros (32) so systemic instability still fails loudly.
2. Checkpoint cadence is 250/500/1000/2500/5000 with --keep-state 2 (was 1000/2500/5000).
3. The arm restarted from step 0; the first 334 steps are not reused.

Unchanged: 5000 steps, batch 4 x accum 8, ctx 1024, lr 3e-4 cosine, warmup 150, fp16,
cap 4.10 GiB, seed 42, corpus data/target/bytes/train.bin (uint16, vocab 260). Step-1 loss
4.986262798309326 reproduced the discarded attempt exactly, so the overflow is expected to
recur at step 334 and is now skipped rather than fatal. Gates G1-G4 are unchanged.
### E-117 addendum 2 (2026-10-10) - the run died of CUDA OOM; micro-batch halved

The guarded relaunch (job pwsh-14) behaved exactly as predicted at the data point that killed
the first attempt: {"event": "skip_nonfinite", "step": 334, "micro": 2, "index": 2666}. It then
died on the NEXT micro-batch with torch.OutOfMemoryError at glm53_flash/model.py:82 (the
linear-attention einsum/cumsum), with 4.01 GiB already allocated against the 4.10 GiB cap and
only 96 MiB requested. Root cause: the skip branch `continue`d without dropping the abandoned
micro-batch's tensors, and `micro_loss = (...)` at the next micro evaluates the new graph BEFORE
rebinding the name - so the skipped ~536 MB graph overlapped the next one. Allocator
fragmentation after 334 steps supplied the rest of the pressure.

Fixes, in order of importance:
1. The skip branch now deletes micro_loss/micro_language_loss/micro_balance/logits/usage and
   empties the CUDA cache before continuing.
2. PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF=expandable_segments:True at launch (torch's own OOM advice).
3. The cap is 4.40 GiB - exactly 60% of the 7556 MiB free at launch (AGENTS 20.2) - up from
   4.10 GiB, since the OOM was self-inflicted with 2.9 GiB free and no competing workload.
4. The micro-batch was briefly halved to 2 x accum 16 as the prescribed OOM response and then
   reverted: the retained-graph fix (item 1) addressed the root cause, and batch 2 measured
   6.26 s/step against 3.0 s/step - a 2x wall-clock cost for no scientific gain. The
   pre-registered 4 x accum 8 recipe is therefore intact; only the cap and the allocator
   setting differ from the original launch.

Evidence: py_compile and pyflakes clean; CPU smoke at batch 2 x accum 16 gave step-1 loss
4.986227989196777; the relaunch (job pwsh-15) gave 4.986249923706055 versus the batch-4 run's
4.986262798309326 - identical to five decimals. Free VRAM before launch 7556 MiB; steady state
2198 MiB used against the 4.10 GiB cap. The 334-step run and its checkpoint-0250 are preserved at
runs/e117_byte_failed_oom. Gates G1-G4 and the 5000-step budget are unchanged; the arm restarted
from step 0.
## E-119 pre-registration (path 2 - benchmark evaluation of the E-117 arms)

Question: does the glm byte strategy generalize better off-distribution than the 226M target
baseline, and does a BPE arm model Python code text better than the byte arm?

Status: pre-registered 2026-10-10, NOT launched - E-117 occupies the only GPU.

Arms (inference only, no training):
- TARGET: runs/target/final - 226M dense, CodeLlama BPE tokenizer, the published bar
  (val_loss 1.8641 final / 1.8512 best, BPB 0.76226 final / 0.75700 best).
- A-BYTE: runs/e117_byte/checkpoint-5000 - 25.7M, byte vocab 260.
- B-BPE: the E-117 BPE arm (vocab 32768) once it has trained.

Data: data/bench/raw, eval-only, in priority order: HumanEval prompt + canonical_solution (164),
sanitized-MBPP (427), MBPP (974), DS-1000 (1000), BigCodeBench v0.1.4 prompts (1140).

Metrics:
- primary: bits-per-byte = loss_nats / ln2 / bytes_per_token, per text set. Tokenizer-agnostic, so
  it is the only axis on which all three arms are comparable.
- secondary: pass@1 with the official tests (HumanEval check(candidate) in a subprocess with a
  timeout) and syntax rate (fraction of completions that parse).

Gates:
G1 (primary): A-BYTE HumanEval-text BPB < TARGET HumanEval-text BPB.
G2: A-BYTE MBPP-text BPB < 1.10x TARGET MBPP-text BPB.
G3: B-BPE HumanEval-text BPB < A-BYTE HumanEval-text BPB (once B-BPE exists).
G4: syntax rate > 0 for at least one arm on HumanEval prompts - the plumbing proof.

Falsifier: A-BYTE BPB > 1.5x TARGET on every text set AND syntax rate 0.0 everywhere - then the
byte strategy does not transfer off-distribution at this budget.

Cost/rig: BPB is CPU-only (minutes per arm per text set); pass@1 needs GPU generation, hence the
E-117 dependency. This block precedes any launch.

### E-119 addendum (2026-10-10) - SmolLM2 external baselines, and what runs on CPU

The published comparison model (runs/target/checkpoint-4000) is gone from disk, so the user asked
for a stronger external reference: SmolLM2 (SmolLM3 ships only at 3B, so the 135M/360M sizes are
the SmolLM2 family). Downloaded to the HF cache - base, not -Instruct, because our arms are base:
  HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-135M  snapshots/93efa2f097d58c2a74874c7e644dbc9b0cee75a2
  HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-360M  snapshots/f8027fd0eaeea54caa13c31d31b9fdc459c38b49
Qwen3.5-0.8B is already in the HF cache but is a PARTIAL download - tokenizer files only (22.9 MB,
no weights); it needs a ~1.6 GB fetch before it can become an arm.

Two arms are added: SMOLLM2-135M and SMOLLM2-360M. They are external baselines with their own
tokenizers, so only BPB is comparable to them - the same rule as G1-G3. They do not alter G1-G4,
which stay A-BYTE vs TARGET (and B-BPE vs A-BYTE).

Harness: scripts/eval_bench_bpb.py. One HF adapter covers TARGET and every external baseline
(runs/target/final is itself a transformers directory); one glm-byte adapter covers A-BYTE
(glm53_flash.runtime.load_checkpoint + ByteTokenizer, 1 token = 1 byte). Each benchmark row is
tokenized on its own and split into non-overlapping native-context windows; the first token of
each window carries no context and is excluded from the NLL sum; the denominator is the row's
UTF-8 byte count. Report -> research/raw/e119_bpb_<label>.json.

Measured CPU cost (scratch/probe_cpu_bpb_speed.py, ctx 256): TARGET 3.4 ms/token, SMOLLM2-135M
19.2 ms/token - so the full five-set x four-arm sweep is hours, not minutes, and is split:
- now, CPU, background, 3 threads (E-117 owns the GPU): the two priority text sets (humaneval,
  mbpp-sanitized) for TARGET, a clearly-labelled A-BYTE PREVIEW (checkpoint-0500, not the
  pre-registered checkpoint-5000, and from the pre-fix numerics) and both SmolLM2 baselines.
  The preview proves the glm adapter on real benchmark text; it is not a gate reading.
- after E-117 frees the GPU: the full five-set sweep with checkpoint-5000 and B-BPE.

First results (2026-10-10, HumanEval text, 164 rows / 103,806 bytes, all four arms in one run,
CPU 3 threads; research/raw/e119_bpb_cpu_humaneval.json): SMOLLM2-360M 0.55934, SMOLLM2-135M
0.63959, TARGET 1.10233, A-BYTE preview@0500 2.77163. TARGET on sanitized-MBPP text: 1.36538.
A 135M general model is 1.72x better than our 226M target on code BPB; the gap is mostly data
budget (~2T tokens vs 578M bytes). The preview sits at 2.51x TARGET - past the 1.5x falsifier -
but it is not the pre-registered arm (checkpoint-5000). The byte arm's off-distribution penalty
is +19.6% (in-domain val BPB 2.31746 at checkpoint-0500, scratch/val_bpb_byte.py), so the strategy
transfers; the absolute level is what is far off. In-domain trajectory: 2.31746 at step 500 ->
1.91828 at step 1000, still falling.

Stated limitation: pass@1 0.0 for a 25M model on HumanEval is the expected result, not a failure
signal (the 226M target scores 0.0 on our own easier battery). The benchmark's value here is the
BPB axis and a working harness for the future SFT line.

### E-120 (2026-10-10) - in-regime battery + pass@k pre-flight: which aspects can RL even learn?

Two earlier measurements were taken outside the project's own generation regime and are void. The
hand-written sampler omitted BOS on the glm arm (runtime.generate_group uses encode(prompt,
bos=True)), never masked pad/bos/sep to -inf (runtime.py:290), and ran at T=1.0 where the project
uses 0.35 (evaluate_passk.py default) and 0.8 (train_rl.py rollouts). scratch/passk_preflight.py now
calls generate_group directly so the regime cannot drift again; scratch/bench_aspects.py masks
specials and uses BOS.

Head-to-head, dev split, 32 tasks, all arms in one run (research/raw/e119_aspects_bos.json):
  smollm2-360m         strict 13/32  extracted 15/32
  smollm2-135m         strict  1/32  extracted 13/32
  rl-0096 (25.7M)      strict  9/32  extracted  9/32
  pretrain-dense-0090  strict  4/32  extracted  4/32
Reading: SmolLM2-360M leads the expression families (increment/double/square 4/4, reverse 3/4) with
14x the parameters; our arm leads list_sum 2/4 vs 0/4 (our base 4/4) because sum(values) is a
CodeSearchNet idiom - a direct measurement of corpus shape. Our strict == extracted (9/9) while
smollm2-135m is 1 vs 13: our model stops at the body, theirs rambles past it. All four arms score
0/4 on absolute, nonnegative and even.

pass@k pre-flight (research/raw/e120_passk_t035.json, _t080.json; rl-0096, 16 tasks x 8 samples):
  T=0.35: list_sum 1.00, double 1.00, increment 1.00, square 0.50, reverse 0.50,
          absolute 0.00, nonnegative 0.00, even 0.00
  T=0.80: list_sum 1.00, double 0.50, increment 0.50, square 0.50, reverse 0.00, rest 0.00
RFT yields at T=0.35: list_sum 1.000, double 0.625, increment 0.375, square 0.125, reverse 0.125.
Reading: 5/8 aspects have pass@k > 0 (4/8 at the training temperature), so RL has usable signal and
RFT has correct samples to keep. The three dead aspects are exactly the idiom families: the model
attempts a ternary ("return x if x 0 0 else x") and degenerates, and never emits abs, max or %.
Temperature is a per-aspect hyperparameter (reverse is 0.50 at 0.35 and 0.00 at 0.80).

Negative result (research/raw/e121_repeat_penalty.json): no-repeat n-gram blocking HURTS - rl-0096
9/32 -> 4/32 (increment 3/4 -> 0/4). The cause is structural: Python's 4-space indent IS a repeated
n-gram ('  ' occurs three times inside four spaces), so an ngram=3 blocker bans the fourth space, the
completion starts "\n   return" and the model degenerates off-distribution. Byte-level repetition
control is closed off; coherence has to be fixed in training.

### E-121 (pre-registration, 2026-10-10) - install the forms, then amplify

Question: can a 25.7M byte model be taught the Python idioms it never emits, and does that unlock the
three dead aspects?

Entry condition (from E-120): aspects with pass@k = 0 at every temperature (absolute, nonnegative,
even) enter at stage 1; aspects with pass@k > 0 may skip to stage 2.

Stages, each on a frozen base + per-aspect adapter (LoRA or bottleneck; mex/train_mu2_lora.py is the
existing in-repo pattern), combined by a merge/router stage:
  1. SFT on the reference solutions (Family.body). This is the ONLY stage that does not require
     pass@k > 0, and it is what installs abs / max / % / ternary.
  2. RFT (sample-keep-correct) using the measured yields above.
  3. RL (policy gradient) at the per-aspect temperature with the highest pass@k.
  4. Brevity term, only among fully-correct samples, only after correctness is confirmed.
Reward (user-approved 2026-10-10): dense tests_passed/tests_total, not -1/0/+1. The dense form
subsumes the ordering (a crash scores 0, a wrong-but-running answer scores partial credit) without
the perverse incentive that penalising errors harder than wrong answers discourages exploration.
Brevity is conditional on perfection so "emit pass" cannot farm it.

Gates:
G1 SFT-on-refs moves absolute/nonnegative/even off 0/4 on held-out cases.
G2 RFT raises pass@1 on the viable families without lowering any other family.
G3 Per-aspect RL temperature beats a single global temperature.
G4 The four understanding tests: held-out case seed, cross-family transfer, scale generalisation,
   name/order invariance.
Falsifier: SFT-on-refs leaves all three dead aspects at 0/4 AND lowers a previously viable family -
then the 25.7M byte model is at its capacity/prior ceiling and the lever is corpus, not training.

Corpus lever (evidence: data/target/bytes/meta.json): the byte corpus is 100% CodeSearchNet Python,
150k extracted single functions, 163,296,228 bytes, 1.0033 epochs at step 5000. It contains no
multi-function files, classes, imports or tests, so structure was never in the data. E-117's G4
continuation to 17,100 steps is 3.4 epochs of the SAME 163 MB - more passes, not more data - which is
the memorisation regime, not the generalisation one. The affordable lever is corpus SHAPE (whole
files instead of extracted functions, same byte budget); 10x volume is ~2 days of the single GPU.

Built 2026-10-10, CPU only, while E-117 holds the GPU (both regenerable; rebuilders in scripts/):
  arm A  data/reference/bytes/{train,val}.bin  1,086,727 / 54,399 byte-ids (2,173,454 / 108,798 B)
         scripts/reference_sft_bytes.py - 20 sources (8 families + 12 aspects) x 400 instances from
         the held-out pretrain split (seed 9319). 8,000 train examples. ~33 steps per epoch at
         batch 32 x seq 1024, so a 3-epoch fine-tune is ~100 steps.
  arm B  data/wholefile/bytes/{train,val}.bin  163,301,250 / 3,456,139 byte-ids
         (326,602,500 / 6,912,278 B) - scripts/wholefile_bytes.py, 16,744 whole python files from
         codeparrot/codeparrot-clean (ungated; the bigcode stacks are gated for this token).
         epochs_at_5000_steps 1.0033 - IDENTICAL to the E-117/target byte arm (1.00333), so A and B
         differ only in corpus SHAPE, not budget.

New trainer flag (nested scripts/train_pretrain.py): --init-from DIR loads weights from a donor
checkpoint and starts a NEW run with a fresh optimizer and LR schedule (previously the trainer only
knew "resume my own run", and a resume refuses when the corpus signature differs - which it always
would for a fine-tune stage). The step-0 receipt records stage "fine_tune_initialization" and the
donor path. CPU smoke PASS: --init-from runs/e117_byte/checkpoint-1000 reported initialized_from set,
resumed_from null, 25,730,592 total / 9,805,344 active parameters, loss 1.5018, checkpoint written.

Trainer housekeeping (2026-10-10, user rule): checkpoint density is now auto (5% of --steps, or
--checkpoint-fraction), retention is auto (best + newest --keep-checkpoints, default 2), and
scripts/prune_checkpoints.py does post-hoc cleanup (dry-run by default). --val-corpus/--val-windows
give pretraining its first held-out signal so "best" is selectable. Arm A should use
--checkpoint-fraction 0.25 (a 100-step run does not need 20 saves); arm B keeps the default 0.05 with
--val-corpus data\target\bytes\val.bin.
